2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消
費されるなか、鉄鋼メーカーによる供給制限が他
の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み
―2027年にかけてフランス大統領選やスペイン・イタリアの総選挙など、欧州主要国で重要な国政選挙が相次ぐ。極右・ポ

市場焦點
美國經濟
• 第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。美國9月個人消
費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，年增3.6%；核心PCE指數月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對
於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，
持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，
將提高川普後續推動政策的難度。觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年
通常是股市表現最佳的年度，其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對

政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
中國
• 9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快，中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI
硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企業則是能
源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲
弱與價格競爭問題未解，投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。

美國第三季GDP成長超預期、通膨壓力續增
• 美國第三季經濟成長率(GDP)創一年來最大增幅：美國經濟分析局(BEA)公布，第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場
估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。企業AI投資火熱、消費者開支保持韌性，抵銷了油價上漲衝擊。
• 9月數據顯示通膨壓力續增，但符合預期：美國9月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，寫3月以來最大月增幅；年增幅
3.6%。剔除食物與能源的9月核心PCE指數，月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 市場反應：由於新一輪通膨數據符合預期，市場未改變聯準會12月利率決議升息一碼的預測。然而，有跡象顯示實質個人
消費支出開始轉弱，儲蓄率也有所下降，市場開始押注通膨可能已經見頂。從聯邦利率期貨市場數據來看，市場對於明年升

息空間的預期已較上個月收斂(九月底預測升息2~3碼→目前預測1~2碼)，
• 觀點：美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，
可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.30。

聯準會貨幣決議與聲明稿差異
資料來源：Federal Reserve（10/28、9/16 聲明）；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。以上為聲明重點整理。
2 / 5
10月決議｜按兵不動
3.75% – 4.00%
10票贊成、2票反對
經濟措辭轉強 「穩健擴張」改為「強勁擴張」；國內支出具韌性， 生產力與資本投資表現強。
通膨仍未解除 聲明維持「通膨居高」的判斷，未再宣稱本次升息有 助加快回到2%目標。
政策訊號更具彈性 改稱需要更多資訊判斷適當政策立場，未承諾下一步 升息或降息。
關鍵變化
理事庫克(Lisa D. Cook) 和克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth M. Hammack)主張本次升息1碼，
形成2張鷹派反對票；9月決議則為一致通過。利率不變不等於轉向寬鬆，委員會內部對通膨風險仍

有不同判斷。
市場解讀：本次是「暫停觀察」；12月是否再升息，仍取決於通膨、需求與能源價格。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
資料來源：Federal Reserve 10/28會後記者會；Reuters、Investing.com，2026/10/28。
3 / 5
01｜市場利率反映緊縮壓力
華許指出，9月與10月兩次會議之
間，美國公債名目與實質殖利率明
顯走高；市場利率可先行反映成長
、通膨與風險變化，不必等政策利
率調整。
政策含意
長天期殖利率仍受通膨預期、期限
溢酬及公債供需影響；政策利率不
變，不代表債市壓力解除。
02｜AI投資與生產力
華許認為美國經濟尚未過熱，AI投
資推升需求，但生產力也有改善；
聯準會將持續評估新科技對產出、
就業與物價的中長期影響。
政策含意
AI支出可支撐企業成長，也伴隨資本
支出回收與融資風險；應檢視現金流
、投資報酬及生產力是否實際提升。

03｜政策制度與工具再檢視
聯準會規劃2027年安排1次、2028
年安排2次專注貨幣政策策略的會
議，不進行利率表決或發布政策聲
明；另將檢視資產負債表政策。
政策含意
制度調整不等於政策轉向寬鬆；仍須
觀察準備金、資產負債表操作及經濟
數據，評估金融條件如何變化。
解讀：華許除談利率外，也聚焦市場利率、AI生產力及政策制度調整；投資判斷仍應回到數據、企業獲利與金融條件。

聯準會利率決議摘要暨投資策略建議
資料來源：Federal Reserve；Reuters、InvestingLive；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。市場價格可能隨後續交易更新。
4 / 5
政策決議 利率維持 3.75% – 4.00% 經濟強、通膨高；政策下一步保留彈性
美股
短線受利率預期與科技股評價牽動
；AI投資支撐獲利，但仍需檢視現
金流與資本支出回報。
美債
若升息預期升溫，短天期殖利率較
敏感；長天期亦受通膨預期、發債
供給與期限溢酬影響。
美元／黃金
美元可能隨利差預期波動；黃金同
時受實質利率、美元及地緣政治避
險需求影響。
投資策略建議｜情境式配置
股票 保留核心持股，避免追價；優先關注獲利能見度、現金流與定價能力較佳企業。
債券 以分散到期日與信用風險為主；可分批配置高品質債券，避免單押長天期利率下行。

多元資產 保留流動性與再平衡空間；若通膨再升、殖利率上行，分批進場並控管整體波動。
後續觀察：美國通膨與就業數據、能源價格、企業財報，以及市場對12月政策的重新定價。

美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政
資料來源： 各媒體，2002~2022年
歷屆期中選舉確認結果時間
選舉日期 眾議院控制 權確認時間 參議院控制權 確認時間 時長
2006年11月7日 選舉日當晚 11月9日 2天
2010年11月2日 選舉日當晚 11月3日 1天
2014年11月4日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2018年11月6日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2022年11月8日 11月16日 11月12日 8天
• 美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政：美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，為川普第二任期上任以來首場全國性選
舉，除國會議員改選外，亦包括州長及其他公職人員選舉。若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，將提
高川普後續推動政策的難度。不過，川普仍可透過發布行政命令等方式，在一定程度上繞過國會的立法制衡，持續推動其政

策議程。
• 本次選舉結果可能耗費一週才能明朗：回顧2006至2022年近五屆美國期中選舉，其中四屆的參眾兩院控制權於投票當晚或
隔日即大致明朗，僅2022年因關鍵席次競爭激烈、郵寄選票計票耗時較長，直至投票後第8天才確認兩院席次歸屬。考量本
次參議院選情膠著，加上川普執政以來美國政治極化持續加劇，不排除本次選舉結果需耗費一週才能明朗，期間市場也可能
面臨額外的不確定性。

期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度
• 期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度：觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳
的年度。根據1940至2025年統計，標普500指數第三年平均報酬率達16.5%，高於第一年的8.3%、第二年的4.2%及第四年
的6.9%；中位數報酬率更達18.9%。其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，
且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
• 政黨與國會組成影響有限，基本面仍是關鍵： 進一步觀察政黨差異，1940年以來，第三年由共和黨總統執政時，標普500指
數平均報酬率約18.2%，高於民主黨總統執政時的14.9%，但兩者均呈現正報酬，顯示總統所屬政黨並非決定股市表現的唯

一因素。國會組成亦可能影響政策推動與市場預期，例如共和黨總統搭配民主黨國會時，第三年平均報酬率約15.6%；若國
會呈現混合執政，平均報酬率則達23.1%。

能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升
• 能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升：9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快。但前三季度累計增速
從1到8月的15.7%小幅放緩至15.5%，利潤改善主要反映了能源和AI硬體價格的上漲，其中AI硬體價格仍是高技術製造業利潤
的重要支撐。前三季度，高技術製造業利潤年成長52.0%，與1到8月54.7%的增速大體相當。拉動整體利潤成長約10個百分點，
貢獻率約三分之二。值得注意的是，利潤成長仍主要集中於少數相關行業，其他企業部門整體依然處境艱難，內需疲弱疊加價
格競爭激烈令利潤受到擠壓。
• 投資建議：中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企

業則是能源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲弱與價格競爭問題未解，
投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.27
能源、AI價格飆升提振中國相關行業利潤增速

中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
• 中國科技公司享受負殖利率差：中國在經濟成長放緩與寬鬆貨幣
政策下，借貸成本持續下降，10年期公債殖利率由年初1.86%降
至1.63%，使企業融資條件更加寬鬆，相較之下，美國受經濟韌
性與高利率政策影響，10年期公債殖利率升至約5%，企業整體融
資成本明顯提高。此外2025年中國大型科技企業在境內債券市場
融資規模有限，僅為約74億美元，而且支付的殖利率，平均比同
風 險 層 級 的 債 券 高 出18個 基 點，但現在溢 價 已 經 變 為 折 價 ，
2026年迄今，中國科技企業已在境內發債約100億美元，新發科
技債殖利率平均折價6個基點，顯示機構投資者信心發生了顯著轉
變，大幅降低了行業推進AI建設的資金成本，會有上述現象是由
於中國經濟通縮，難以承受更高利率，因此官方須維持較寬鬆的

金融條件，科技企業剛剛開始通過債券市場融資，預料債券將受
到投資者關注。
• 展望後市：低成本債券融資對中國科技行業構成利好，與IPO不
同，債券市場為企業提供了一個可重複利用的融資渠道，在股市
劇烈波動之際，對投資人是另一種資產配置，可優先關注具資金
優勢與應用場景的大型科技龍頭，以及受惠於AI算力需求成長的
伺服器、網通設備和資料中心產業。
中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
中美國債走勢分化
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.29

AI產業鏈融資警訊：資金缺口正在擴散
• 資本支出與現金流的嚴重失衡：AI生態系中的巨額投資正考驗著企業，一旦企業營運現金流不足以支持基礎設施建設，就必
須高度依賴外部融資(發債或增資)，使其更容易受到資本市場波動與利率變化的衝擊。AI算力提供商資金缺口最為顯著，其
資本支出已接近營運現金流3倍(284%)，需要投入大額資金採購高端GPU並對外出租算力，數據中心資本支出佔現金流的比
例已從2022年11月(ChatGPT問世)1倍(104%)快速爬升至1.3倍(128%)，超大規模雲服務商資本支出占現金流比例已從2022
年底40%翻倍飆升至73%。在彭博研究統計的8家超大型雲業者中，出現自由現金流為負(資金缺口)公司數量已從2022年的
1家增加至4家(達50%)。
• 產業鏈錯綜複雜，風險具連鎖效應：從上游晶片製造商、硬體開發商、建築商、電力/公用事業，到中游的數據中心與雲端

服務商，再到下游的軟體與數據管理公司，其銷售與支付合約彼此高度緊密相連。當算力提供商或數據中心出現資金吃緊時，
風險會迅速向上游傳遞，導致項目延期、融資成本攀升，甚至延後整個生態系的收入實現時程。目前除了雲端與數據中心外，
硬體開發商與建築商中出現資金缺口的企業比例也在上升。
資金缺口正蔓延至人工智能產業鏈一半的層級
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
算力提供商和數據中心的資本支出超過現金流

市場定價轉變：借貸成本上升與股票估值修正
• 資本市場已經開始針對資金缺口進行風險定價：在資金缺口最大的AI算力提供商、數據中心以及超大規模雲服務商中，其發
行的公司債相較於美國國債的殖利率差(利差)顯著拓寬，高於一般投資級債券或高收益債券的表現，顯示債券市場對其違約
與融資風險要求更高的風險補償。由於大量現金流被基礎設施建設掏空，且AI應用端的獲利回收速度未達市場預期，投資人
不再願意給予極高溢價，整體AI產業鏈的預期本益比出現明顯修正。
• 小結與投資建議：當前AI產業正在經歷基礎設施過渡期，資本密集的算力出租、數據中心建設正遭受強烈的資金擠壓，若未
來1~2年內下游應用端的商業化營收無法順利銜接，依賴高度槓桿與外部融資的企業將面臨嚴峻的流動性挑戰。對AI算力出
租商及部分高負債的數據中心開發商保持謹慎態度，一旦信貸市場收緊或借貸成本升高，利潤與營運將受重創。晶片製造商

的經營現金流持續成長，財務結構改善，選擇擁有強大訂價權與現金流護城河的上游半導體巨頭，安全邊際最高，電力與公
用事業資本支出佔現金流比例相對穩定且受惠於剛性需求。超大規模雲服務商中表現差異大，優先選擇資產負債表健全、資
產回報率高且擁有傳統核心業務能持續提供穩定現金流的科技巨頭，遠離需大量靠發債維持資本支出的個股。
超大規模雲服務商和數據中心的利差擴大
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
隨著借貸成本波動加劇，AI股權估值走弱

顧 • 利率可能維持高檔更久，但收益價值正在浮現：近月以
來美國10年期公債殖利率一度升抵5.36%，30年期接
近5.7%，反映市場重新評價未來利率中樞、財政赤字
及資金需求等因素。雖然長天期債券價格仍可能受到利
率波動影響，但高殖利率已大幅提高新進場資金的收益
起點，投資焦點應由預測利率轉向評估收益價值。
• 債券標售與資金流顯示配置需求逐步回溫：10月美國
財政部10年期、20年期及30年期公債標售需求維持穩
健，投標倍數約介於2.5至2.8倍區間；同時，美國債券
基金已連續26週呈現資金淨流入，顯示市場未必認為
利率已見頂，但已開始接受5%以上殖利率的長期配置
價值。
• 收益優先取代押注利率轉折：在Higher for Longer環
境下，債券的核心功能重新回到穩定收益、分散風險與

降低組合波動。建議可以3至7年期美國公債及高評等
投資級公司債作為核心收益部位，長天期債券則採分批
布局方式，兼顧收益來源及未來利率循環變化帶來的潛
在機會。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

科技股漲多修正，資金輪動主導盤勢
2026 年 8 月
7 月全球股市呈現鮮明的區域輪動，資金由先前漲幅集中的亞洲 AI 供應鏈，轉向估值與今年以來
表現相對落後的市場。以美元計價觀察，MSCI 中國、東協及拉丁美洲為當月前三強。相較之下，
MSCI 新興亞洲與新興市場跌幅較大，MSCI 新興亞洲中南韓與台灣合計權重接近六成，7 月南韓半
導體及 AI 硬體股因估值偏高、資本支出回收疑慮與槓桿部位平倉而急跌，成為指數的主要壓力來源。
即便 7 月新興亞洲與新興市場出現回檔，但年初迄今表現仍佳，反觀中國與印度仍分別下跌 8.0%及

9.0%。因此，我們認為 7 月盤勢較接近強勢 AI 題材暫時性獲利了結，而先前落後市場低基期補漲的
資金輪動行情，尚不足以判定全球股市原有多頭結構已全面反轉。
七月全球債市整體偏弱，僅新興當地債及亞洲高收益債維持正報酬。以美元計價觀察，新興美元
債下跌 1.30% 為最弱券種。當月債市壓力主要來自能源價格波動、通膨黏著性及聯準會政策不確定
性。Fed 於 7/29 決議將利率維持在 3.50%至 3.75%，但三位地區聯準銀行總裁主張立即升息一碼，
官方聲明亦明確指出通膨仍高於 2%目標，能源供給衝擊持續推升部分價格。當日會後 30 年期美債
殖利率創 2007 年以來高位，但 2 年期殖利率卻下降，反映市場並非單純押注短期升息，而是提高對
長期通膨、政策可信度與期限溢酬的要求，殖利率曲線因而明顯陡峭化。
我們認為，七月股市修正主要來自折現率、評價與籌碼調整，目前尚未出現 AI 需求下降的證據。
半導體類股近期修正後，評價壓力已部分釋放，但技術面仍未形成明確底部。既有核心持股不宜因短
線波動而全面減碼，但建議降低高評價、高擁擠度及高度依賴遠期獲利族群的集中度。此外，投資人

可以定時定額或在拉回時分批布局具獲利潛力的台、日股市。韓國股市則受三星電子、SK 海力士雙
巨頭合計占指數權重逾半，個股風險易被結構性放大，建議投資人於短線反彈適度調節相關曝險。
在聯準會減少前瞻指引、利率路徑更依賴數據環境下，市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將提
高，預期債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較大
波動，且存續期間過短亦面臨再投資風險；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給
增加等負面因素。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸
引力的息收，亦能降低短端政策波動及長端期限溢酬風險。

美國股市：AI 成長主線未變，短線估值風險升溫
七月美股受伊朗戰爭不確定性、聯準會態度轉鷹干擾，股市出現巨幅震盪。雖財報盈餘表現強勁，
但市場開始觀察資本支出擴張壓迫科技龍頭企業的現金流。指數上揚主要由微軟、蘋果、亞馬遜、輝
達、埃克森美孚、博通、摩根大通銀行、雪佛龍、萬事達卡、威士卡等企業帶動，表現相對弱勢的族
群包括美光、特斯拉、英特爾、應用材料、科林研發、科磊、晟碟、超微半導體、卡特彼勒、康寧等。
美國經濟整體仍維持穩健，但第二季 GDP 年化季增率由 2.1%放緩至 1.5%，主要反映進口增加、
企業庫存調整及政府支出下降。民間消費仍是第二季經濟成長的主要支撐，年化季增率由第一季的

0.5%大幅回升至 3.2%，其中耐久財消費成長 6.8%、非耐久財成長 4.4%，服務消費亦由 0.5%回升
至 2.2%。此外，第二季民間投資成長 3.0%，其中非住宅投資成長 8.4%，顯示 AI 資料中心、軟體與
研發支出仍持續擴張。另一方面，第二季進口大幅成長 11.5%，增幅主要集中於電信設備、半導體及
工業設備等資本財，反映企業資本支出需求仍相當強勁。
能源價格回落有助通膨降溫，但整體物價壓力仍不容忽視。6 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 月增率同步放
緩，其中整體 PCE 轉為月減 0.1%，反映能源價格明顯回落，足以抵銷其他商品與服務價格上漲壓力，
年增率為 3.7%，仍高於 Fed 目標。核心 PCE 月增率亦降至 0.1%，創近數月低點；其中，聯準會高
度關注的超級核心服務通膨出現降溫，主要受投資組合管理費漲幅由 4.5%大幅降至 0.6%，以及旅館
住宿與機票價格回落帶動。不過，醫療保健價格仍維持穩健增長，且部分商品價格持續受到關稅影響
而上揚，加上美伊戰事仍未落幕，通膨風險依舊存在。

本輪由 AI 投資帶動的「AI 通膨」，對整體 CPI 的直接推升有限，卻有助於川普推動「美國製造」
政策目標。2024 年以來，全球雲端服務業者大幅擴張 AI 資本支出，帶動高階晶片、記憶體需求急升，
不過，電視、電腦周邊及手機合計占美國 CPI 權重偏低，即使相關產品價格上漲，對整體通膨的直接
影響仍相對有限。相較之下，AI 通膨對產業結構與供應鏈在地化的影響更值得關注。台積電毛利率
上升同時也擴大在美國資本支出、美光也在記憶體價格回升後重新啟動紐約廠建設、輝達則進一步投
資光通訊與先進封裝業者，協助相關產能在美國落地。唯有當價格與需求長期維持在足以支撐獲利的
水準，企業才有能力承擔高額資本支出，並推動製造業供應鏈回美擴張。
目前標普 500 指數續創新高，本益比已升至 25.8 倍、股價淨值比達 5.8 倍，短期評價偏高。雖
然美國科技業者目前仍居 AI 產業領先地位，但 AI 技術與競爭格局變化快速，領先廠商尚未與後續追
趕者明顯拉開差距。短期仍需留意市場過熱與估值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：經濟溫和復甦，評價仍具吸引力
七月歐股受伊朗戰爭不確定性干擾而震盪。泛歐 600 指數中，能源與金融股表現相對強勢，包括
殼牌、匯豐、道達爾能源、BP、畢爾巴鄂比斯開銀行、聯合聖保羅銀行及法國巴黎銀行等；相較之
下，半導體、科技硬體與部分防禦型消費股表現偏弱，包括艾司摩爾、英飛凌、意法半導體、諾基亞、
雀巢等。整體市場焦點由科技股回歸能源與金融族群。
歐元區第 2 季 GDP 初值季增率回升至 0.4%，顯示經濟動能在第 1 季停滯後逐步修復。不過，成
員國表現仍明顯分化：西班牙季增 0.7%，持續領先主要經濟體，主要受惠於服務業復甦與觀光需求

回升；德國 GDP 季增率則放緩至 0.2%，反映製造業疲弱與出口需求不足仍限制復甦力道；法國則
由前季萎縮轉為成長 0.2%，顯示內需逐步改善。整體而言，第 2 季 GDP 回升主要受消費改善、投
資回穩及政府支出支撐，但復甦力道仍屬溫和。
7 月歐元區通膨率受能源價格反彈影響，小幅回升至 2.9%，已連續 5 個月高於 ECB 的 2.0%目標，
但仍低於 5 月 3.2%的高點。核心通膨則升至 2.5%，主要受服務業與非能源工業品價格同步走揚帶
動，其中服務業通膨由 3.2%升至 3.3%，非能源工業品由 0.7%升至 0.9%；食品與菸酒年增率則續降
至 1.2%，反映食品價格降溫趨勢延續。整體而言，物價壓力僅溫和回升，尚未出現全面加速跡象。
ECB 在七月利率決策會議維持政策利率 2.25%不變，重申將採取逐次會議、數據依賴的決策模
式，不預先承諾未來利率路徑，官方仍維持經濟成長風險偏下行的評估。雖然歐元區整體通膨較前期
高點放緩，但中東衝突再度升溫，推升原油與天然氣價格，加上目前全球能源與天然氣庫存水位偏低，

未來能源價格變化及其對通膨的傳導效果，仍將是市場關注重點。ECB 刻意未恢復前瞻指引，並持續
強調後續數據的重要性，反映其希望在高度不確定環境下保留政策彈性，因此不排除 9 月升息的可能。
7 月歐元區綜合 PMI 回升至 51.9，重返榮枯線之上並創 5 個月新高，整體景氣動能有所改善。
其中，服務業 PMI 升至 51.6，受惠於需求回升而重返擴張區間；製造業 PMI 亦由 51.4 升至 51.9，
加上新訂單 5 個月來首度恢復成長，進一步支撐景氣修復。
歐洲經濟整體呈現溫和復甦，泛歐 600 指數成分股多為具備全球布局優勢的產業龍頭。目前指數
本益比約 17.5 倍、股價淨值比約 2.5 倍，評價仍屬合理。雖然能源價格高檔與通膨再升溫風險仍需
留意，但在評價合理、伊朗戰爭有望於第三季落幕的背景下，歐股仍適合作為長線分批布局標的。

日本股市：日圓匯率短線急升，股市評價合理
七月日股受地緣政治風險與全球科技股回檔影響，日經 225 指數劇烈震盪。盤面上，金融、外銷
與大型權值股表現相對強勢，包括三菱 UFJ 金融集團、日立製作所、索尼、豐田、三井住友金融、
東京海上控股、瑞穗金融、三菱商事等；半導體與電子零組件族群則相對承壓，包括東京威力科創、
村田製作所、鎧俠、瑞薩、Disco、信越化學等。整體市場焦點由科技股轉向外銷股與升息預期受惠
的金融股。
7 月東京 CPI 年增率自前月的 1.7%攀升至 2.0%、東京核心 CPI（扣除生鮮食品）年增率為 1.9%、

扣除食品與能源後的 CPI 年增率則為 1.3%。商品價格年增率由前月的 2.3%升至 2.9%，是通膨回升
的主要原因；服務價格年增率則小幅升至 1.2%，顯示薪資成長仍推升服務價格。分項來看，本月通
膨上行主要來自能源與公用事業費用，反映電費與瓦斯費價格上漲；同時，食品價格年增率也由前月
的 3.8%回升至 4.0%。展望後市，日本政府 7~9 月重啟電費補貼，將抑制能源價格。不過，近期國
際油價再度攀高，可能抵消部分政策效果。
日本央行七月利率決策會議維持政策利率不變，委員以 8 比 1 同意將政策利率維持於 1.0%。僅
高田創委員主張將政策利率調高至 1.25%。就景氣現況來看，日本經濟仍呈現溫和復甦態勢，儘管部
分經濟活動受到中東局勢影響而略顯疲弱，但出口與工業生產大致保持平穩。受惠於全球 AI 相關需
求強勁成長，企業固定投資仍溫和增加；同時，私人消費在就業與所得環境改善支撐下仍具韌性。日
銀也將 2026 財年 GDP 年增率預估由前次的 0.5%小幅上調至 0.6%，2027 財年由 0.7%上調至 0.8%。

近期美日聯手干預日圓匯率，日本財務大臣片山皋月表示，已協同美國財政部實施買進日圓的
干預措施，以因應近來日圓匯率過度波動及失序走勢，且不排除進一步採取協同干預。美國財長貝森
特也表示，將毫不猶豫參與進一步的聯合日圓干預行動，並強烈支持日本採取果斷措施，修正日圓嚴
重低估的問題。美國總統川普則稱，日本需要一些幫助。此次為日本與美國 15 年來首次就日圓採取
聯合行動，兩國共同干預匯市確實對日圓形成支撐，突襲式干預也有助引發投資人空單回補，暫時延
續日圓升值趨勢。不過，目前日美政策利率差距仍高達 2.5%，若日本央行無法加快升息步伐，中長
期匯價重新回歸利差基本面，日圓仍可能重拾偏弱格局。
2026 年來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、高市擴大財政刺激與強化軍事安全等政策利多支撐，
儘管指數已明顯上漲，目前本益比約 19.5 倍、股價淨值比約 1.8 倍，評價仍屬合理，可利用市場震
盪機會逢低分批布局。

台灣股市：AI 出口與接單動能續強，8 月聚焦財報兌現與供應鏈擴散
7 月台股因前期漲幅較大、科技股評價偏高，加上市場重新檢視 AI 投資回收期、企業獲利能否符
合高預期，外資調節、融資去化與槓桿資金停損進一步放大波動，資金也由高評價電子股轉向較具防
禦性的族群。不過，這波修正有助消化過高評價與浮動籌碼，降低市場過熱風險；後續隨雲端業者財
報持續驗證 AI 資本支出，半導體、伺服器及先進封裝需求延續，市場焦點可望逐步回歸企業獲利與
產業基本面。6 月景氣對策信號升至 41 分、連續第 7 個月亮紅燈，製造業銷售與加班工時同步轉強，

顯示 AI 動能已由出口延伸至生產、設備投資與勞動投入。雖然產業間景氣感受仍有落差，但下半年
電子產品備貨需求、半導體資本支出及 AI 基礎建設持續推進，仍可望支撐出口與企業獲利，為後續
盤勢提供正向動能。
當前臺灣經濟正處於生產擴張的黃金期。最新公布的總體經濟數據與半導體龍頭營收呈現全面共
振，印證全球 AI 資本支出（CapEx）並非流於故事題材，而是實質轉化為台廠的訂單與獲利。儘管
全球金融市場短期受到地緣政治干擾與美債殖利率波動影響，引發台股在高檔震盪，但主計總處大幅
上修 2026 年 GDP 預測至 9.64%。國發會公布 6 月景氣對策信號綜合判斷分數為 41 分，較 5 月
增加 2 分，連續第 7 個月呈現代表景氣熱絡的紅燈，為史上第二長的連續紅燈紀錄，顯示台灣景氣
並非單月跳升，而是已形成一段相對持續的高檔擴張週期。
展望 8 月，台灣景氣仍有望維持高檔，但景氣分數的邊際變化將比燈號本身更值得關注。全球
AI 基礎建設持續擴張，加上下半年進入消費性電子備貨旺季，預期有利半導體、AI 伺服器、先進封

裝、散熱、電源及高速傳輸供應鏈；國內主要半導體業者提高資本支出，也將持續帶動設備、材料及
廠務工程需求。另一方面，景氣分數已接近紅燈區間上緣 45 分，未來即使出口與生產維持強勁，受
到比較基期墊高影響，分數未必持續上升，因此不宜將分數持平或小幅回落直接解讀為景氣反轉。
投資策略上可維持對 AI 供應鏈的正向看法，但選股宜從題材轉向獲利品質。除先進製程外，8
月可提高對先進封裝、測試介面、AI 伺服器組裝、散熱、及記憶體的關注。IDC 預估 2026 年全球半
導體市場規模將達 1.29 兆美元、年增 52.8%，資料中心半導體營收達 4,771 億美元，意味著 AI 投資
正由 GPU 延伸至記憶體、網路與系統級零組件。不過，若股價已提前反映高成長，8 月仍應留意財
測不及預期、匯率、成本上升及資金集中度過高所引發的波動。

中國股市：復甦力道偏緩，科技自主為結構性題材但仍待驗證
7 月中國經濟延續「總量放緩、科技製造相對抗跌」的分化格局。2026 年上半年 GDP 年增 4.7%，
第二季成長率由第一季的 5.0%放緩至 4.3%；6 月規模以上工業增加值年增 5.3%，其中製造業成長
6.0%，電腦、通信及其他電子設備製造業成長 15.7%。數據顯示 AI 與電子設備提供局部支撐，但內
需疲弱與房地產調整仍未見明顯改善，整體復甦動能偏緩，成長結構對少數產業的依賴度偏高。
科技產業方面，7 月市場焦點轉向半導體設備、AI 模型與記憶體的自主化，惟題材面成分仍高於

基本面。據媒體報導，中國已開始製造國產浸沒式 DUV 微影設備，首批系統預計供應中芯國際、華
虹半導體及長鑫科技，但 2026 年產量可能僅約 5 台，且效能、可靠度與量產驗證仍明顯落後 ASML，
短期象徵意義大於實際產能貢獻。後續能否降低成熟製程與部分多重曝光製程對進口設備的依賴，仍
取決於良率與客戶端驗證結果，對國產蝕刻、薄膜沉積、檢測及零組件的實質拉動亦需時間觀察。
AI 軟體端，月之暗面於 7 月推出 Kimi K3，模型具 2.8 兆參數、100 萬 Token 上下文及原生視覺
理解能力，並採開放權重策略，反映中國大模型競爭已由低價推論延伸至模型能力與開發者生態。惟
開放權重的商業模式尚未證明可持續獲利，應用落地速度亦存在不確定性。若相關應用推展順利，或
有利國產雲端服務、資料中心、伺服器與推論晶片需求；但模型效率提升同時可能壓抑算力採購規模，
市場對 AI 投資的檢視標準將轉向使用效率與變現能力。
記憶體方面，長鑫科技正由一般 DRAM 逐步向 DDR5、LPDDR5X 及 HBM3 延伸，市場研究「估

計」其 2026 年底 DRAM 月產能可能接近 35 萬片晶圓，惟該預估未經證實，實際良率與產品組合仍
待確認。國產 DUV 若逐步導入其產線，或可提高設備取得的自主性，但導入時程與穩定度均具變數。
整體而言，8 月中國市場可留意半導體設備、國產記憶體、AI 模型、資料中心與工業自動化，惟評價
已部分反映預期，投資判斷宜以技術驗證、訂單落地與實際營收貢獻為依據，避免過度追逐自主替代
題材。
展望 8 月，政策環境預計仍以穩增長及培育新動能為主，但政策效果傳導有限，市場表現料將持
續分化。中國上半年新動能，包括高端製造、數位經濟與現代服務業，對經濟成長的貢獻超過四成；
第三季製造業雖有 AI 相關產品、綠色科技出口及政策支持，惟房地產、基礎建設投資及中小企業需
求疲弱仍是主要壓抑因素。

拉美市場：巴西通膨改善支撐市場表現，惟總統大選不確定性仍存
7 月全球股市普遍面臨回檔，然而拉美股市表現卻相對抗跌，主因巴西市場先前已因降息預期收
斂而出現修正，而近期巴西通膨數據及通膨預期出現改善，使市場對於巴西通膨問題的擔憂有所緩
解，也帶動巴西指數表現相對硬挺，並為整體拉美市場指數提供支撐。
7 月巴西通膨數據出現改善，主要來自食品與燃料價格回落。7 月巴西 IPCA-15 消費者物價指
數月增 0.06%，明顯低於市場預期的 0.22%，年增率亦由 6 月的 4.80% 降至 4.52%。其中，家庭
食品價格由 6 月月增 0.87% 轉為月減 1.14%；燃料價格整體月減 0.90%，汽油價格月減 0.68%，

帶動整體通膨放緩，並降低市場對近期巴西物價再度急升的疑慮。
此外，本次巴西通膨數據改善亦反映在核心通膨與服務通膨上，使優於預期的數據更接近結構性
通膨改善。五種核心通膨指標平均月增 0.21%，年增率由 4.45% 降至 4.35%。同時，服務通膨月
增 0.41%，低於市場預期的 0.59%，年增率也由 6.25% 降至 5.95%；然而，勞力密集型服務年增
率仍由 7.06% 升至 7.30%，顯示通膨壓力尚未全面消退，後續仍需持續觀察。
另一方面，儘管巴西通膨數據改善，仍未逆轉市場對巴西央行降息幅度受限的預期。截至 7 月 27
日的巴西央行每週市場調查顯示，2026 年市場通膨預期較前期下降 3 個基點至 5.12%，但 2027
年仍上升 2 個基點至 4.22%，2028 年亦連續第二週上升 2 個基點至 3.80%，2029 年則維持
3.50%；四個年度皆持續高於巴西央行 3.0%的通膨目標。此外，市場對 2026、2027、2028 及 2029
年底巴西基準利率的預期，亦分別維持在 14.00%、12.00%、10.50%及 10.00%，顯示市場對通膨

的疑慮仍限制巴西利率正常化的速度與幅度。
除了通膨之外，巴西總統大選是影響拉美市場的另一項關鍵因素。巴西民調機構 Nexus 於 7 月
全國民調顯示，在總統大選第一輪中，魯拉以 42% 領先波索納洛的 33%，與 7 月中旬前次調查
的 42% 對 34% 大致相同；第二輪則為 47% 對 43%，魯拉仍領先 4 個百分點。兩輪調查均由
魯拉領先，顯示其整體勝率仍較高。此外，包含 Datafolha、Genial／Quaest 等民調皆顯示目前魯
拉贏面較高。由於魯拉並非市場偏好的候選人，其較高的連任機率可能壓抑巴西股市表現。因此，儘
管近期巴西通膨降溫有助支撐整體拉美市場行情，巴西選舉風險仍可能抵銷部分利多；同時，在美伊
衝突仍具不確定性的情況下，巴西降息行情亦料將受到壓抑，後市表現預期將落後於全球市場。

科技類股：CSP 廠商資本支出持續加碼，市場更加關注獲利轉化能力
全球科技產業的主要成長動能仍集中於 AI 基礎建設，四大雲端服務供應商資本支出均維持高檔
或進一步上修，顯示資料中心、AI 伺服器、網路設備及雲端容量的擴建週期尚未降溫。Microsoft、
Amazon 與 Google 的雲端業務均維持強勁成長，且訂單、產能需求與客戶使用量持續增加，反映企
業導入生成式 AI、主權 AI 及 AI 工廠的趨勢仍在深化。市場焦點也逐漸由單一 AI 晶片出貨，延伸至
高速網路、記憶體、CPU、電力與散熱等整體資料中心架構。只要 CSP 廠商未明顯下修資本支出，

AI 硬體供應鏈的訂單能見度便可望維持，NVIDIA、AMD 及相關半導體與設備廠商仍是本輪科技投
資循環的主要受惠者。
不過，市場對科技公司的評價標準正由投入多少資本支出轉向能否將投資轉化為營收、獲利與現
金流。Microsoft 在大幅增加資本支出的同時，營運現金流與自由現金流仍維持成長，Google 雲端
業務的獲利也明顯提升，說明部分 AI 投資已逐步進入商業化階段；Amazon 則受惠 AWS 成長加速，
雲端業務持續扮演主要獲利來源。相較之下，Meta 與特斯拉因 AI 研發、基礎建設及新事業投入快
速增加，面臨費用上升、利潤率下降與自由現金流承壓的問題。未來科技股表現可能進一步分化，市
場將更重視 AI 收入成長、產能利用率、投資回收期及折舊費用，而非單純將高額資本支出視為利多。
從供應鏈角度來看，AI 需求正由 GPU 逐步擴散至 CPU、記憶體、晶圓代工、類比晶片與工業半
導體。Intel 資料中心與 AI 業務明顯加速，顯示 CPU 在 AI 伺服器的資料處理、系統控制與通用運算

架構中仍具重要地位。記憶體價格上升雖有利於相關製造商，但也推高 CSP 建置成本，促使雲端業
者重新檢視採購效率與系統配置。整體而言，科技產業有機會由少數 AI 核心企業領漲，逐步轉為伺
服器、網通、儲存、電源、散熱及工業半導體共同受惠的擴散型成長。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但高成長也伴隨高估值與高資本密集度風
險。NVIDIA 的 Blackwell 出貨、Rubin 平台時程、高階記憶體供應與中國市場需求，將是觀察 AI
硬體循環的重要指標；Apple、Meta 與特斯拉則需證明 AI 功能、新裝置及自動化應用能夠轉化為實
際收入。目前 CSP 訂單與雲端需求仍未出現明顯降溫，科技產業基本面維持正向，但隨著折舊、能
源、記憶體及研發成本陸續上升，企業獲利差異可能擴大。未來較具優勢的企業，將不只是擁有先進
技術或積極投入 AI，而是能建立穩定商業模式，並將 AI 投資有效轉化為營收、利潤與現金流的公司。

能源類股：美伊衝突帶動油價波動，實體供應仍未見改善
7 月油價波動劇烈，主因美伊衝突與停火談判反覆。美伊在 7 月中旬恢復互相攻擊、美國重啟
對伊朗港口的海上封鎖，且荷莫茲海峽再度發生商船遇襲事件，帶動布蘭特原油一度重返每桶 100
美元以上；然而其後美方暫停攻擊並重啟外交談判後，油價又回落至約 80 美元，這顯示當前油價
變動主要反映市場對於衝突升降溫的預期，而非實際供應已恢復正常。
彭博分析指出，目前石油實物市場尚未出現明顯改善，荷莫茲海峽可觀察到的油輪數量仍遠低於
戰前，許多船隻通行時仍持續關閉船舶自動識別系統，且保險公司、租船人及船東也維持審慎態度，

顯示商業航運、海運保險、運費及出口流量均未恢復正常。航運資料公司 Windward 7 月 30 日的
船舶追蹤也顯示，荷莫茲海峽當日僅有 7 艘船舶進入波斯灣、4 艘離開，整體運量相較於戰前仍有
極大的落差，故一旦美伊談判受阻，地緣政治風險溢價仍有迅速回升的可能性。
另一方面，胡塞武裝宣布將封鎖沙烏地阿拉伯有關的船舶運輸，並對紅海曼德海峽通行發出威
脅，也加劇油價上行風險。彭博指出，沙烏地阿拉伯雖可利用東西輸油管，將原先在荷莫茲海峽受阻
的每日約 500 萬桶原油運往紅海沿岸的延布港，但經由紅海運往亞洲的船舶仍須通過曼德海峽，因
此原油出口風險只是由波斯灣轉移至紅海。船舶追蹤公司 Windward 截至 8 月 3 日的統計顯示，
7 月 20 日後曼德海峽船隻流量下降 22%，油輪更減少 40%，顯示經紅海的替代路線仍面臨威脅。
然而，高油價帶來的需求破壞也會限制油價上漲空間。OPEC 7 月月報將 2026 年全球石油需求
量下修至每日 1.0594 億桶，需求年增量由每日 97 萬桶降至 78 萬桶；2027 年需求年增量雖上

修至每日 194 萬桶，但上修主因 2026 年基期較低的緣故，並非終端需求出現改善。
在此背景下，彭博研究將油價分為三種情境：若外交協議落地、荷莫茲航運與保險恢復正常，布
蘭特原油可能回到每桶 65 至 80 美元；若戰事僅暫停、實物供應持續受阻，則較符合每桶 90 至
110 美元的衝突僵局情境；若荷莫茲與曼德海峽同時受阻，油價可能升至每桶 140 美元以上。
展望 8 月，川普宣布將和伊朗展開新一輪談判，儘管隨即遭到伊朗否認，且荷莫茲海峽仍處於受阻
狀態，然而我們認為隨著期中選舉時程逼近，川普將可能暫緩對伊朗的行動，藉此壓低油價挽救選情，
在此情境下能源類股將會轉趨區間震盪整理，目前中性看待後市表現。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：央行配置需求仍強，惟 ETF 資金流出壓抑短線表現
7 月金價保持在區間內震盪整理，儘管美元走強及美債殖利率攀升持續為金價帶來壓力，然而來
自全球央行的買盤也如我們先前所料的為金價帶來了有力的支撐。
世界黃金協會報告指出，2026 年第二季，全球黃金總需求為 1,269 噸，與 2025 年同期持平；
累計 2026 年上半年，全球黃金總需求年增 2%至 2,522 噸，需求金額創下 3,800 億美元新高。與此
同時，2026 年第二季倫敦金銀市場協會黃金午後定盤價平均為每盎司 4,506.29 美元，雖較第一季下

跌 8%，但仍較 2025 年同期上漲 37%。這顯示第二季金價雖自高檔回落，但全球黃金需求並未同步
轉弱，尤其央行買盤回升更成為支撐市場的主要力量。
第二季全球央行合計購買 289 噸黃金，歷經第一季的放緩後，購金規模再次重新回到過去四年
的高檔水準。高盛分析則預估，全球央行 5 月購金量達到 81 噸，其中中國購買 48 噸，是當月最大
的可識別買家；經季節調整後，全球央行近三個月月均購金量達到 67 噸，約為 2022 年前每月 17
噸平均值的四倍。上述數據顯示，即使在金價走勢低迷的狀況下，全球央行對於黃金的承接力道仍強。
進一步觀察央行持續購金的原因，官方貨幣與金融機構論壇（OMFIF）2026 年針對 74 家央行
及 16 家公共退休與主權基金進行的調查顯示，儲備多元化仍是央行持有黃金最主要的原因。此外，
有 51%的受訪者將防範地緣政治風險列為考量，比例較 2024 年增加 11 個百分點；同時，79%的受
訪央行認為全球貨幣體系正朝向多極化發展。調查結果顯示，各國央行正逐步降低對單一儲備貨幣的

依賴，並增持黃金以因應制裁、資產凍結及貨幣體系分裂風險，由此也可見，本波全球央行買盤更接
近長期儲備調整，而非短線對於金價的追逐。
然而對於利率較為敏感的私人投資資金，降低黃金配置趨勢仍持續為金價帶來明顯壓力。世界黃
金協會數據顯示，2026 年第二季全球黃金 ETF 淨流出 45 噸，6 月單月再流出 89 億美元；其中，北
美黃金 ETF 上半年累計流出 77 億美元。造成北美資金流出的主因，在於聯準會態度偏向鷹派，加上
美伊衝突推升通膨疑慮，帶動實質利率與美元走高，進而提高持有黃金的機會成本。
展望後市，儘管短線上金價表現持續承壓，然而中長線看，當前市場已定價到明年底前 Fed 將
升息 2 碼的鷹派走勢，若後續美伊衝突有所緩和，使市場升息預期降溫，黃金 ETF 資金賣壓有望出
現改善，屆時搭配央行長期買盤，黃金仍有望重啟新一波漲勢，故 我們對黃金類股後市維持中性看法。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：Fed 鷹派暫停推升長率，投資仍以 3~10 年期美債/投級債為主
FOMC 7/29 以 9 比 3 票將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.50%～3.75%。Hammack、Kashkari
及 Logan 三名委員反對維持利率不變，主張本次即升息 1 碼。會後聲明指出美國經濟活動仍以穩健
速度擴張，資本投資及生產力成長強勁，但通膨仍高於 2%目標，部分反映能源等供給衝擊。
Fed 主席 Warsh 強調，本次按兵不動並非聯準會行動遲緩或政策慣性，並重申不存在高於 2%
的「軟性通膨目標」。超過五年的高通膨不可能因單月物價小幅下降便獲得解決，聯準會將持續履行

物價穩定責任，必要時不會猶豫採取行動。華許同時指出，距離上次會議的 42 天內，美債殖利率曲
線各期限的名目與實質殖利率均明顯上升，部分升幅已位居近 20 年的前十分位數。他認為，聯準會
減少前瞻指引後，市場開始依據經濟數據自行調整價格。此番談話並非表示聯準會受制於市場，而是
暗示金融市場利率上升已產生部分緊縮效果，使本次不必立即升息。華許指出，AI 相關高科技設備
及軟體投資近四季增長率接近 20%，有助於製造業產出及未來生產力，但記憶體、邏輯晶片及相關
AI 基礎建設價格亦同步上升。FOMC 已開始討論，相關漲價究竟只是特定產業的供給衝擊，或可能
演變為更廣泛的通膨壓力。
債市對此次 FOMC 決議的反應呈現長端領跌、殖利率曲線明顯陡峭化。此一走勢顯示，市場雖
因 Fed 未立即升息而降低短端利率壓力，卻同步提高對能源通膨、財政供給、期限溢酬及政策可信
度的風險定價。利率期貨對 9 月升息的定價在決議後一度升至約 75%，稍後回落至約 6 成，代表市
場並未排除升息，而是等待後續通膨與就業數據確認。8 月沒有例行 FOMC 利率會議，債市定價將

更加依賴經濟數據。由於 Warsh 減少前瞻指引，單一數據對升息機率及短端殖利率的影響可能較過
去放大，預期 8 月殖利率仍將維持高檔震盪。
8 月債市的核心矛盾在於「殖利率已具吸引力，但長端風險尚未完全穩定」。我們認為偏高的殖
利率可提供較佳息收及價格緩衝；若油價維持高檔、核心通膨降溫停滯或美國公債供給增加，20～
30 年期債券仍可能承受期限溢酬上升壓力。貝萊德亦指出，目前美債實質殖利率已具吸引力，但收
益機會仍應透過存續期間控制與券種選擇取得，而非全面延長年期。
在未來聯準會減少前瞻指引、利率路徑更依賴數據環境下，市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將
提高，預期債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較
大波動，且存續期間過短亦面臨再投資風險；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供
給增加等風險。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸
引力的息收，亦能降低短端政策波動及長端期限溢酬風險。

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 260,000원

현재 주가(10/6) 112,600원 
상승여력 ▲130.9% 
시가총액 439,605억원 
발행주식수 390,413천주 
52 주 최고가 / 최저가 206,000 / 100,600원 
90 일 일평균 거래대금 1,184.39억원 
외국인 지분율 39.1%

주주 구성  
현대자동차 (외 6 인) 37.0% 
국민연금공단 (외 1 인) 6.8% 
기아차우리사주 (외 1 인) 2.3%

주가수익률(%) 1개월 3개월 6개월 12개월 
절대수익률 -11.0  -29.9  -25.7  8.1  
상대수익률(KOSPI) -14.8  -16.1  -53.1  -87.5  
(단위: 십억 원, 원, %, 배) 
재무정보 2024 2025 2026E 2027E 
매출액 107,449  114,141  128,720  138,081  
영업이익 12,667  9,078  9,832  11,157  
EBITDA 15,216  11,792  13,055  14,580  
지배주주순이익 9,773  7,561  8,043  8,891  
EPS 24,575  19,457  20,765  22,942  
순차입금 -15,303  -19,162  -21,973  -24,762  
PER 4.6  5.8  5.4  4.9  
PBR 0.8  0.7  0.7  0.6  
EV/EBITDA 1.9  2.1  1.7  1.3  
배당수익률 5.8  6.0  6.2  6.7  
ROE 19.1  12.9  12.7  12.9

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 YoY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 YoY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지
면서 전년 동기 수익성 악화에 크게 영향을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조지아 텔루라이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스포티지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수요가 높은 북미 현지에서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중심의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추정치는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부정적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것으로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상반기 적용한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목표주가를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상대적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모멘텀에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

### Page 1

2026년 10월 29일 
기업분석

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

### Page 2

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
内容提要 
2026 年 10 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较前月下降 0.2 个百分点，供给充足食品价格继
续下行，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，震荡前行。10 月能源价格继续上涨，对当前 CPI 增
速形成主要的拉升因素。 
2026 年 10 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格继续
上涨，尤其能源价格的大幅上涨，推动工业品价格增速继续上行。一方面，8 月以来的美伊
战争加剧，国际能源价格再次上涨；另一方面，促消费叠加整治“反内卷”式竞争，耐用消
费品价格增速明显上涨。 
10 月通信工具价格同比增长 16.1%，较 9 月上涨 1.8 个百分点，对本月 CPI 增速形成拉
升因素；交通工具用能源价格同比增长 20.2%，较 9 月上涨 5.3 个百分点，亦对 CPI 增速形
成主要的拉升因素。与外贸进出口、PPI、工业企业利润等指标相契合，通信工具价格持续
上涨，价格增速高位运行，主要受科技大爆发，终端需求旺盛推动的价格不断上涨，是经济
结构调整的阶段性结果。

图 1 CPI、PPI 当月同比增速（%） 图 2 CPI、PPI 环比增速（%） 
数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 数据来源： Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心

正  文 
需求推动，通信工具价格继续上行 
2026 年 10 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较前月下降 0.2 个百分点，供给充足食品价格继
续下行，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，震荡前行。10 月能源价格继续上涨，对当前 CPI 增
速形成主要的拉升因素。

### Page 3

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
本月食品价格同比下降 2.9%，降幅较前月下降 1.2 个百分点，是导致本月 CPI 增速小
幅下滑的主要因素。10 月果蔬供给充足，鲜果、鲜菜价格同比下降 3.8%、16.9%，降幅分别
较 9 月扩大 2.3、8.8 个百分点，是拉低本月 CPI 增速的主要因素之一。 
10 月通信工具价格同比增长 16.1%，较 9 月上涨 1.8 个百分点，对本月 CPI 增速形成拉
升因素；交通工具用能源价格同比增长 20.2%，较 9 月上涨 5.3 个百分点，亦对 CPI 增速形
成主要的拉升因素。与外贸进出口、PPI、工业企业利润等指标相契合，通信工具价格持续
上涨，价格增速高位运行，主要受科技大爆发，终端需求旺盛推动的价格不断上涨，是经济
结构调整的阶段性结果。

图 3 CPI 食品与非食品当月同比增速（%） 图 4 CPI 食品与非食品环比增速（%） 
数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 
食品项中，从同比看，食品价格增长-2.9%，较前月下降 1.2 个百分点，供给充足使得食
品价格同比继续负增长。总体而言，食品项八大类价格同比分化（1 涨、7 跌、0 持平 ），其
中鲜菜价格同比幅度最大（-16.9%）；较上月同比变动分化（3 正、4 负、1 不变），其中鲜
菜价格同比变动最显著（-8.8%）。

食品价格环比增速的主要因素，黄金价格下降明显是导致本月环比增速下滑的主要因素。总
体而言，非食品项七大类价格环比分化（3 正、2 负、2 持平），其中其他用品及服务价格环
比幅度最高（-1.1%），较上月环比变动分化（1 正、4 负、2 不变），其中衣着、教育文化
娱乐价格环比变动最明显（-0.6%、0.6%）。

资源争夺叠加供需不匹配问题缓解，2026 年价格增速或有上行 
CPI 方面，深入整治“反内卷”式竞争或能缓解供给相对过剩的问题，同时受“稳增长、
促消费”政策影响，叠加 2025 年的低基数效应，或推动 2026 年 CPI 增速上行。经济内生动
力不足，国内经济压力依然较大，就业、收入预期并未发生明显改变，居民有效需求不足问
题掣肘，抑制居民消费价格短期快速大幅上涨，但若中东战争的影响持续扩大，或加速通胀
率的上行。综合预计 2026 年 CPI 增速或温和上涨。 
PPI 方面，一方面，国内外环境进一步复杂多变，能源、矿产争夺加剧，或推动上游原
材料价格上涨，政策调整，“依法依规治理企业低价无序竞争”，叠加低基数效应，拉动 2026
年 PPI 增速上行；另一方面，中国国内经济压力犹存，企业内生动力不足，工业有效需求有
待刺激，房地产市场产能去化犹在，经济结构调整继续，抑制需求端扩张。但若中东战争的
影响持续扩大，或产生输入性通胀，工业品价格上涨或加速。综合预计 2026 年 PPI 增速或
震荡上行。

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
北京大学国民经济研究中心简介： 
北京大学国民经济研究中心成立于 2004 年，挂靠在北京大学经济学院。依托北京大学，重点研
究领域包括中国经济波动和经济增长、宏观调控理论与实践、经济学理论、中国经济改革实践、转轨
经济理论和实践前沿课题、政治经济学、西方经济学教学研究等。同时，本中心密切跟踪宏观经济与
政策的重大变化，将短期波动和长期增长纳入一个综合性的理论研究框架，以独特的观察视角去解读，
把握宏观趋势、剖析数据变化、理解政策初衷、预判政策效果。 
中心的研究取得了显著的成果，对中国的宏观经济政策产生了较大影响。其中最具有代表性的成
果有：（1）推动了中国人口政策的调整。中心主任苏剑教授从 2006 年开始就呼吁中国应该立即彻底
放弃计划生育政策，并转而鼓励生育。（2）关于宏观调控体系的研究：中心提出了包括市场化改革、
供给管理和需求管理政策的三维宏观调控体系。（3）关于宏观调控力度的研究：2017 年 7 月，本中
心指出中国的宏观调控应该严防用力过猛，这一建议得到了国务院主要领导的批示，也与三个月后十
九大报告中提出的“宏观调控有度”的观点完全一致。（4）关于中国经济目标增速的成果。2013 年，
刘伟、苏剑经过缜密分析和测算，认为中国每年只要有 6.5%的经济增速就可以确保就业。此后不久，
这一增速就成为中国政府经济增长速度的基准目标。最近几年中国经济的实践也证明了他们的这一测
算结果的精确性。（5）供给侧研究。刘伟和苏剑教授是国内最早研究供给侧的学者，他们在 2007 年
就开始在《经济研究》等杂志上发表关于供给管理的学术论文。（6）新常态研究。刘伟和苏剑合作的
论文“新常态下的中国宏观调控”（《经济科学》2014 年第 4 期）是研究中国经济新常态的第一篇学术
论文。苏剑和林卫斌还研究了发达国家的新常态。（7）刘伟和苏剑主编的《寻求突破的中国经济》被
译成英文、韩文、俄文、日文、印地文 5 种文字出版。（8）北京地铁补贴机制研究。2008 年，本课
题组受北京市财政局委托设计了北京市地铁运营的补贴机制。该机制从 2009 年 1 月 1 日开始被使
用，直到现在。 
中心出版物有：（1）《原富》杂志。《原富》是一个月度电子刊物，由北京大学国民经济研究中
心主办，目的是以最及时、最专业、最全面的方式呈现本月国内外主要宏观经济大事并对重点事件进
行专业解读。（2）《中国经济增长报告》（年度报告）。该报告主要分析中国经济运行中存在的中长
期问题，从 2003 年开始已经连续出版 14 期，是相关年度报告中连续出版年限最长的一本，被教育部
列入其年度报告资助计划。（3）系列宏观经济运行分析和预测报告。本中心定期发布关于中国宏观
经济运行的系列分析和预测报告，尤其是本中心的预测报告在预测精度上在全国处于领先地位。

Document 353: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.
